### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 歐洲央行上周四(9/10)為年內第二次上調利率，以應對能源價格上漲帶來的通膨
壓力 ， 並警告物價壓力可能比先前預期更久 ， 推動市場加大對後續進一步收緊政
策的押注 。 歐洲央行總裁拉加德形容此次决定爲”無需多想的决定”， 並警告通膨
回落至 2%目標的時間點目前預計在 2027 年底。歐洲央行預期明年平均通膨爲
2.5%，高於 6 月的 2.3%；2027 年核心通膨預期爲 2.6%，高於此前的 2.5%。
⚫ 軍事消息人士稱，與伊朗結盟的胡塞武裝上周四(9/10)奪取葉門港口城市的控制
權 ， 並沿紅海海岸向南推進至戰略島嶼地區 。 美國總統川普表示 ， 他預計伊朗戰
爭將在美國中期選舉後結束。若胡塞武裝控制這條與 荷姆茲海峽同樣重要的水
道 ， 其支持者伊朗將在與美國的戰爭中獲得關鍵戰略優勢 ， 並進一步推高油價 。
⚫ 美國勞工部勞工統計局上週四(9/10)公布 8 月生産者物價指數 PPI 年增 5.4%，

高於市場預期的 5.3％。週五(9/11)公布 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 3.4%與前
月相同，核心 CPI 年增率 2.4%符合市場預期，月增率達 0.3%高於市場預期。
FED WATCH顯示數據公布後推升市場對於聯準會本周將升息的預期。
⚫ 日本央行數據顯示，8 月國內企業物價指數較去年同期上漲 7.6%。增幅高於市
場預估中值 7.4%。7 月漲幅經修正後爲 7.7%；8 月進口物價指數同比上漲 24.8%，
7 月修正後爲上漲 29.3%， 表明當月日圓貶值以及中東局勢影響推高進口成本和
整體通膨，同時鞏固市場對日本央行在 9 月會議進一步升息預期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Sep/14/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.455，低點在 31.698，以 31.638 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 0.8 分。
上週台幣先升後貶，週初，受到市場對於日本央行 9 月升息的預期升溫，日圓
走升帶動美元指數跌破 99 整數價位低檔震盪 ， 加以台股高檔震盪 ， 外資延續匯入的
走勢，帶動台幣升破31.500 至 31.455 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月生產者物
價指數年增率由上個月的 4.8%上升至 5.4%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示聯準會
9 月升息機率攀升至 7 成，美元指數回到 99 上方，台股高點回落，外資偏向匯出，
台幣由升轉貶至 31.698 週內低點，終場在央行調節之下收在 31.638。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣區間震盪。國際市場方面，上週五公布美國 8 月核心消費者物價
指數月增率 0.3%高於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，提高本週聯準會升息的機率

至接近 9 成，此外中東局勢仍有升溫風險，油價持續高檔震盪，美元指數在 99 整數
關口盤整；就台灣市場來看，台灣 8 月出口衝上 824 億美元，創下歷年單月新高紀
錄，月增 9.4%，年增 41%，展現極為強勁的成長動能。顯示受惠於 AI 需求全面爆
發，帶動外銷出口暢旺，基本面強勁持續支撐台幣的表現；盤勢上，匯價逢低吸引
進口商及政府基金買盤進場，逢高則面臨出口商賣美金，且本週台美均有利率決策
會議，預估匯率波動將加劇。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間震盪。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.400-31.900

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/14/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週人民幣震盪走升。週初，雖然美伊戰火未平，帶動油價及美債利率高漲，
但市場對於日本央行 9 月升息預期升溫，日圓偏升帶動美元指數跌破 99 價位後低
檔震盪，人民幣延續偏升走勢，走升至6.7022 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月
生產者物價指數 5.4%優於市場預期 5.3%， 加以8 月核心消費者物價指數月增率 0.3%
優於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示本週聯準會 9 月升息預期大
幅攀升至近 9 成，美元指數回到99 上方，人民幣回吐部分週初以來的漲幅，終場境
外人民幣收在 6.7071，週線升值 8 點，境內人民幣則收在6.7079，週線升值 29 點。  
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間偏升。國際市場方面，美國通膨數據表現優於市場預期，
加以油價攀升至每桶 100 美元上方 ， 美債殖利率位居高檔 ， 美元指數也在99 上方盤
整，關注本週聯準會最新利率決議，預計在會議結果出爐以前，聯準會升息預期將
支撐美元指數，限制人民幣升值的空間。中國國內方面，人行中間價格緩步偏升，
上週升破 6.7800 價位，持續釋放人民幣偏強信號，支撐人民幣延續升值的方向。技
術面方面 ，USD/CNH 匯價居於各天期均線下方 ，且均線向下發散 ，加以匯價由低點
向上反彈測試 6.7150 關鍵價位後面臨壓力，顯示空方動能較盛，預計人民幣將延續
升值的態勢。綜合以上，預估本週人民幣走勢區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/14/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌 。週初 ，因市場預期日本央行將加快升息步伐及資金將
回流日本，使 USD/JPY 跌破至 155 整數關卡之下；週間，USD/JPY 延續跌勢最低
來到 152.87 後反彈，後續大致在 153 至 154 間波動；臨近週末，美國公布 8 月 PPI
及 CPI 數據顯示通膨仍處高位 ，USD/JPY 一度反彈至154.6 後回落 ， 終場收在153.54，
週線大跌 270 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週公布日本第二季 GDP
較上季增長 0.4%，季增年率也由 1.1%上升至 1.4%，主因為企業資本支出改善，而
7 月名目薪資年增 4.7%為 30 年來最大漲幅，而實質薪資年增 2.4%也為 5 年多來最
大漲幅，顯示日本經濟復甦力道較原先預期強勁，進一步強化市場對於日央升息的
預期，有利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月 CPI 及 PPI 數據顯示美國通膨仍處高位，強化
市場對聯準會升息預期，且中東局勢升溫，帶動油價突破 100 美元，預期將支撐美
元，限縮 USD/JPY 下行空間。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價多次向
下測試 153 整數關卡未能有效突破，顯示下方有買盤支撐，而 155 關卡附近則為前
波壓力位，因此短線先以區間看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的
可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：152.00-157.00 
前週交易區間 
152.87 156.27

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Sep/14/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 小幅下跌。 週初 ，EUR/USD 大致在 1.1600 至 1.1635 窄幅波動；
週間，市場等待歐洲央行利率決議及 美國通膨數據，EUR/USD 持續在 1.1615 至
1.1653 波動；週四晚間公布歐洲央行利率決議如預期升息 1 碼，而美國 8 月 PPI 高
於預期使市場對聯準會本月升息預期上升，EUR/USD 下行跌破 1.16 整數關口；臨
近週末，美國公布 8 月 CPI 顯示通膨仍具韌性，帶動 EUR/USD 走低來至 1.1568，
終場收在 1.1598，週線下跌 15 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週歐洲央行公布利率決
議如預期升息一碼，總裁拉加德表示即使面對能源價格衝擊，歐洲經濟第二季依然
展現韌性，預期第三季仍會延續，但未來通膨仍面臨上行風險，偏鷹派言論使市場
對於歐洲央行未來持續升息的預期上升，將帶給歐元支撐。二、美國 8 月 PPI 月增
0.4%符合市場預期 ， 年增5.4%高於預期 ，而 8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%年增 3.4%符合預期 ，
顯示通膨仍維持高位，使市場對聯準會升息預期增加，將支撐美元，不利歐元匯價
上行。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖一度突破 1.1630 年線但未
能站穩，顯示上方仍有賣壓，而下方1.1550 附近則有支撐，在有效突破前以區間震
盪看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1530-1.1680 
前週交易區間 
1.1568 1.1653

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                         新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.07-115.09.11 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.69% 
公債－10 年期-A15106 1.92 % ~ 1.921% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.822% ~ 1.823%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連日減發定存單 ， 使市場資金呈現平穩態勢 ， 且
資金需求不若先前，銀行間交投較為清淡，令短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.69%，利率較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週 5 年期指標券 A15108 成交在約 1.82%，10 年期指標券 A15106 成交在約
1.92%。 
美伊衝突升溫，帶動國際油價持續走揚，加劇市場對通膨之疑慮，加上美國8
月非農就業人數大增、8 月 PPI 年增率高於市場預期，市場預估聯準會下週升
息機率升至七成，帶動美債利率大幅反彈；台灣央行將於下週召開理監事會
議 ， 在台灣經濟成長與通膨維持高位下 ， 台灣央行面臨緊縮壓力 ， 預期台債殖
利率面臨走升壓力 ， 預估5 年券區間為 1.83%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.93%~2%。

市場焦點
美國
• Fed褐皮書顯示，美國經濟整體維持溫和擴張，10個地區經濟活動小幅至溫和成長，消費及企
業需求仍有支撐 ，但住宅市場偏弱 、消費者價格敏感度升高 。就業僅小幅增加 ，薪資溫和成
長；另一方面 ，能源、運輸、原物料及關稅成本持續推升企業支出 ，物價在多數地區溫和上
升，反映經濟韌性與通膨壓力並存，增加Fed政策判斷難度。
美伊衝突
• 美國８月底宣布對逾 60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁 ，試圖切斷伊朗的石油 、金融與貿易收入 。
但伊朗歷經數十年制裁後 ，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡 ，但短期預期美國仍難迫使德黑
蘭屈服。對金融市場而言 ，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口 ，油價上行空間相對有限；反
之，若美國擴大次級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期。

韓股
• 儘管市場波動劇烈 ，韓國企業在第⼆季度財報季的盈利前景仍在持續改善 ，在三星電⼦和 SK
海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一
度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整體來看，今年韓國盈利預期累計上調幅度接近 190%，並推動整
個新興市場盈利預期提升約 37%，晶⽚需求指引依然強勁 ，隨著盈利預測持續上調 ，台灣市
場也從中受益。

美國製造業維持擴張，需求降溫與高成本壓力牽制擴張動能
• 新訂單降溫，製造業維持擴張韌性 ：美國8月ISM製造業PMI降至54.6%，較7月55.6%回落，但仍連續第8個月位於擴張
區間；新訂單降至 53.7%、訂單積壓降至 51.8%，顯示需求動能較前月放緩 ，惟生產指數仍達 58.3%，連續第10個月擴
張，運輸設備、機械及石油與煤炭等主要產業維持成長，製造業整體仍具韌性。
• 生產韌性延續，價格壓力仍偏高：就業指數降至51.2%，雖較前月走弱但仍處擴張區間；供應商交貨指數升至59.3%，代
表供應商交期進一步拉長 ，而價格指數維持71.1%，原物料價格已連續 23個月上升，鋼鋁、關稅及石化產品成本仍是主
要壓力來源。整體呈現「需求降溫、生產偏強、成本仍高」的格局，企業利潤與通膨傳導仍需觀察。

• 後市觀察：製造業景氣仍具韌性 ，但新訂單放緩與投入成本高檔並存 ，後續應觀察需求能否延續 ，以及鋼鋁、能源與關
稅成本是否進一步轉嫁至終端價格上 。投資布局可聚焦半導體 、電機電⼦及AI基礎建設等受惠於製造投資與 AI資本支出
的領域；債券則以中短天期及投資等級公司債為主，降低利率波動風險，以股債配置分散景氣與利率風險。
ISM製造業PMI關鍵分項指標比較

褐皮書揭美國經濟溫和成長，成本壓力增添Fed政策難度
• 經濟溫和擴張，消費與企業需求仍有支撐：Fed褐皮書
顯示，自7月初至8月下旬，10個地區經濟活動小幅至
溫和成長、2個地區持平，消費支出整體小幅增加 ，製
造業、部分服務業及金融活動有所改善 ，資料中心與
國防相關需求亦提供支撐；但住宅市場表現仍偏弱 ，
消費者對價格更加敏感 ，顯示經濟維持韌性但內需表
現分化。
• 就業增幅有限 ，成本壓力仍牽動通膨 ：就業整體僅小
幅增加，7個地區就業成長 、5個地區大致持平 ，薪資
多數維持溫和上升；然而能源 、運輸、金屬、石化原
料及關稅成本仍持續推升企業投入成本 ，部分企業甚
至難以完全轉嫁成本，整體價格仍呈現上升。
• 市場觀察： 褐皮書反映美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但就

業降溫與通膨壓力並存，使Fed政策取向更具挑戰。近
期市場對9月升息的預期明顯升溫 ，政策仍高度依賴後
續通膨與就業數據 。建議債券配置宜以短中天期美債
及投資級公司債為主 ，降低長天期利率波動；股票則
聚焦大型優質科技 、AI基礎建設及高現金流企業 ，並
搭配黃金分散通膨與地緣政治風險。

制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服
上海合作組織(SCO)佔全球經濟(GDP)的四分之一
資料來源：IMF，Bloomberg，2026.9.1，其他上海合作組織成員國包括巴基斯坦、伊朗、哈薩克、烏茲別克、
白俄羅斯、蒙古、吉爾吉斯、塔吉克和阿富汗資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.8.26
伊朗絕大部分石油銷往中國
• 制裁聲勢強，但關鍵仍在中國：美國８月底宣布對逾60個伊朗相關實體實施制裁，試圖切斷伊朗的石油、金融與貿易收入。
制裁能否真正奏效，關鍵仍在於是否將中國納入主要打擊對象。伊朗近八成的石油銷往中國，美方現階段僅將少數中港企業
列入黑名單，刻意避開了中國大型國有金融與能源巨頭，若強行實施全面制裁，可能危及關鍵礦產供應、破壞脆弱的美中貿
易休兵，甚至推升全球能源價格。

• 制裁使伊朗經濟受創，仍難迫使政權屈服：伊朗歷經數十年制裁後，已建立相對成熟的制裁規避網絡，並透過中國、俄羅斯、
印度及中亞國家參與的上海合作組織（SCO）等非西方平台維持經貿往來。俄羅斯與伊朗合作持續深化，巴基斯坦亦表態無
義務遵守美方制裁，印度則在制裁壓力下維持一定程度的經貿交流。因此，美國新一輪制裁雖將加劇伊朗經濟壓力，但短期
仍難迫使德黑蘭屈服。對金融市場而言，若制裁未進一步衝擊伊朗石油出口，油價上行空間相對有限；反之，若美國擴大次
級制裁並波及中國等主要買家，則可能推升原油價格與全球通膨預期，進而延後聯準會降息時程，增加股債市場波動。

歐元區通膨率升至近三年新高，９月升息機率高
• 歐洲央行9月升息機率高：歐洲央行（ECB）9月升息至2.50%的預期已逐步升溫，主因歐元區8月通膨升至3.3%，加上能源
價格受伊朗戰事推升，強化鷹派對進一步緊縮的主張。不過，核心通膨降至2.4%、服務通膨趨緩，且長期通膨預期仍大致穩
定，顯示能源價格尚未明顯向薪資與核心物價擴散，因此，9月升息後暫停仍是較可能的政策路徑。 9月升息後，ECB是否於
12月再次升息，將取決於能源衝擊的持續時間及其向核心通膨的傳導程度。
• 12月是否再升息，能源價格是關鍵：若伊朗戰事延續、油氣價格維持高檔，鷹派可能推動利率於12月進一步升至2.75%；反
之，若能源價格回落、服務通膨持續降溫，則本輪升息可能在9月告終。整體而言，ECB政策已轉向偏鷹，但在缺乏能源衝

擊廣泛傳導至核心通膨的明確證據下，年內連續升息空間有限。在政策利率逐步觸頂的情況下，歐債殖利率若因升息預期而
上行，反而可提供較佳的中長期配置與收益機會。
歐元區通膨（CPI） ECBspeak指數暗示歐洲央行還會進一步加息
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.2 資料來源：，2026.902

韓企業績展望在第⼆季財報加速改善
• 韓企業績展望在第⼆季財報加速改善 ：儘管市場波動劇烈 ，韓國企業在第⼆季財報的 獲利前景仍在持續改善 ，在三星電⼦和
SK海力⼠公佈業績後，MSCI韓國指數的全年每股純益預期被小幅上調 ，此前5至6月修正步伐一度放緩，如今⼜重新加快，整
體來看今年韓國獲利預期累計上調幅度接近 190%，並推動整個新興市場 獲利預期提升約37%，晶⽚需求指引依然強勁 ，隨著

望成為第三季韓股上漲的重要動能，尤其是記憶體產業龍頭企業，擁有充沛的自由現金流，實施庫藏股買回，不僅能提升每股
價值，也反映管理層對未來獲利前景具信心，並認為目前股價被低估，參考日本推動公司治理改革後帶動股市重評價的經驗 ，

預期韓股後市仍具支撐力，若市場出現短期回檔，反而可視為逢低布局的機會。

潛力，投資回報能見度提升，在政策支持、技術自主及商業化加速三大因素帶動下 ，AI概念股若因短期波動出現回檔 ，仍可
視為中長期布局機會。

顧
美債市場結構性轉變：從不挑剔的機構到追求回報的私人投資者
• 自新冠疫情以來 ，美國10年期公債殖利率持續走高 ，過去市場多半歸咎於聯準會升息或通膨 ，但彭博最新研究顯示 ，期限
溢價的顯著上升才是主要推手，其背後的根本原因是美債買家發生了結構性轉變。
• 傳統非價格敏感型買家縮手：過去幾十年，聯準會(透過QE)與外國央行是美債的兩大底氣來源，他們買美債是為了儲備與流
動性，對價格不敏感，因此能有效壓低利率與期限溢價 。自2020年以來，聯準會與外國官方持有的美債佔美國 GDP比重分
別下降12%與8%，光是這兩大買家的退場，就推升10年期美債期限溢價約1.05%。
• 私人投資者接棒，市場對殖利率極度挑剔： 目前的邊際買家已轉為外國私人投資者 (如基金、保險公司等)，私人買家會精算

美債、公司債、股票與各國主權債的收益差異，進行嚴格比價。當美債發行量增加時，必須給予更高的殖利率才能吸引他們
購買，這使得美債利率波動更加劇烈。
自疫情以來，長期利率呈上升趨勢
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
主要機構買家持有的美國國債減少

供給端新挑戰：AI浪潮帶動公司債暴增，搶奪資金池
• 科技巨頭發債融資潮 ，公司債成為美債的強勁替代品： 受人工智慧建設資本支出帶動 ，大型科技企業 (如Alphabet、Meta、
Amazon等)積極發行投資級公司債 。這些高品質科技公司債提供優異回報 ，吸引了大量私人資金從美債轉向公司債 。為了
與這些高收益的高評級公司債競爭資金，美國國債殖利率面臨更大的上行壓力。
• 小結及建議：當前美債殖利率居高不下，核心原因在於穩定的買家減少，而接手的私人買家變得挑剔，加上AI熱潮創造了高
回報的公司債競爭源。在美國財政部發債壓力未減的情況下，長端殖利率短期內難大幅下行。債券策略建議採取配置中短天
期債券，長天期公債(10年期以上)價格受期限溢價與供給壓力壓制，波動較大，建議縮短持債年期(如布局3-5年期)，鎖定高

殖利率的同時降低價格波動風險 ，加碼高品質投資級公司債，目前私人資金偏好高評級公司債(尤其是科技與AI龍頭債)，這
類債券信用風險極低，收益率卻優於同天期公債，是當前固定收益資產的優質選擇。
外國私人投資者購買美國公債和公司債
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.1
人工智慧相關債務發行只是殖利率上升的原因之一

日圓再現匯率干預跡象 套利交易平倉恐加劇日圓升值
• 日圓再現匯率干預跡象：上週週間，日圓兌美元匯價出現單日大幅升值情況，市場再度懷疑官方出手干預匯率。然而，彭博
指出，日本央行帳戶數據沒有大規模買入日元跡象，意味著匯率的劇烈波動可能來自市場重新調整日本央行加息預期，而非
日本政府再次入市干預。與此同時，美國財政部並未回應是否參與干預。
• 市場懷疑干預之際，套利交易平倉可能正在發生：儘管無法證實官方上週是否直接干預日圓匯價，但日圓走升可能也反映上
週日本央行官員高田創釋出鷹派升息的態度，將不排除一次性大幅升息或連續升息的可能性。根據芝加哥商品交易所的數據
顯示，日圓融資的套利交易正在平倉，而且中期內持有日圓而非其他G10貨幣的意願非常強烈。
• 觀點：2024年8月日本央行意外升息，引發日圓套利交易平倉，全球主要國家股市短期間出現修正，當時AI與半導體成為套

利資金撤離重點。本次匯價波動暫時可歸咎於套利交易倉位的調整，尚未出現恐慌賣壓，重點將關注在本月中的日本央行利
率會議，留意意外大幅升息的風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.3。

• 今年年初黃金價格創下歷史新高，突破每盎司5,000美元大關，成為金融市場最受關注的資產之一，隨後經歷幾個月的高檔
修正後，上個月再度獲得市場資金追捧，現貨黃金價格單月大漲近10%。過去市場認為黃金上漲主要來自地緣政治風險與央
行降息預期，但近期投資邏輯似乎正在改變。
• 黃金多頭始自央行「去美元化」：本輪黃金多頭始自2022年烏俄衝突，西方國家凍結俄羅斯央行的大部分海外美元與歐元
外匯儲備，讓各國央行開始意識到外匯儲備的安全性，進而推動外匯儲備多元化。透過適度減持美元(美債)來佈局實體黃金
或其他國家貨幣，被成為「去美元化」趨勢。
• 「貶值交易」有望接棒黃金上漲動能：上個月美國公布最新國債規模已突破40兆美元，市場開始擔憂財政赤字的可維持性，
貨幣信心下降導致「貶值交易」發生(美債信心下降)，除了導致長天期美債殖利率上揚之外，貨幣避險需求再度讓黃金資產

成為市場焦點，8月份現貨黃金一度上漲超過15%，隨後雖因聯準會釋出鷹派訊號，單月漲幅仍接近10%。
貶值交易趨勢下，黃金的投資機會

• 央行買盤仍為黃金中期表現帶來利多：根據世界黃金協會2026年各國央行調查，上半年截至5/19，有45%的央行預期未來
一年將增加自身黃金儲備，創調查9年來新高。央行增持黃金主要考量包含：儲備多元化、美元走弱預期、地緣政治衝突以
及金價長期上漲趨勢，顯示黃金已成為各國儲備資產的重要戰略配置，在強勁的需求買盤下，多數機構預測黃金將維持多頭
至明年年底。
• 觀點：近期黃金價格反彈後，可能會受到獲利了結以及利率政策預期變化而出現波動，但在全球債務問題、貨幣貶值風險及
央行買盤支撐下，在中期內仍有機會走出慢牛行情。建議投資人以資產配置的角度，逢修正適度配置黃金資產(如：黃金ETF)。
風險接受度較高的投資人，則可關注金礦類股，但需注意其對於美元匯、利率的敏感度較高，資產價格波動較大。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

EQUITY: TECHNOLOGY
SDI Corporation   2351.TW 2351 TT
Global Markets Research
11 September 2026
Stronger power lead frame outlook in 2H26-2027F
Heat spreader products the cherry on top; maintain Buy
We see further 2026/27F growth potential from power lead frame and heat spreader
Our recent industry channel checks suggests: 1) stronger power lead frame demand, 
especially from AI datacenter related in 2H26-2027F: As mentioned in our previous note, 
global integrated device manufacturers’ (IDM) 3Q26E sales outlook continues to show strength 
(3Q26E weighted average revenue y-y of +17% / q-q of +7%, vs +18% / +11% in 2Q26; this 
implies the fifth quarter of y-y expansion since 3Q25). Specifically, a few of SDI’s key IDM 
customers at their 2Q26 earnings calls raised 2026-27E AI datacenter revenue with stronger 
demand and faster capacity expansion (two customers’ 2027E-related sales should double y-y, 
according to our channel checks). We expect SDI’s 2027F power lead frame revenue from AI-
related field to realize 134% y-y growth (15% of total sales vs 8% in 2026F), with 60 ongoing 
projects currently; and 2) favorable pricing and product mix leading to better 2026/27F 
margin profile: Given supply tightness across several end markets, we expect further pricing 
improvement for SDI in 2H26F, after two rounds of price hikes in 1H26. In addition, given 
stronger demand from auto and AI datacenter (with GM better than other end markets), we 
expect SDI’s 2H26F/FY27F GM to reach 22.1%/23.0% vs 20.0% in 1H26.
2026-27F to mark the milestone of heat spreader business development
In addition to continued capacity expansion for power lead frame products, SDI’s management has 
decided to add more stamping equipment (ranging from 100-ton, 200-ton, to500-ton) in 2026-27E 
to handle increasing demand for its newly-developed product – heat spreader (including hat, cavity, 
ring/stiffener, and micro-channel lid, or MCL). The company will focus on automotive, ASIC/CPU, 
and networking in AI datacenter fields. Given superior manufacturing capability (20-30µ coplanarity 
vs 50-70µ of industrial average), it has secured four major orders from OSAT, auto, and IC design 
customers, with management expecting major revenue contribution from 2027E (we estimate 
3%/7% of 2027/28F total sales). We believe there can be sales and earnings contribution upside 
once we are able to confirm more order receiving from higher-end products.  
Raise 2026/27F EPS assumptions by 8%/17%; TP raised to TWD327
We raise 2026/27F sales and earnings growth (Fig. 1) with stronger order placing from global 
IDM customers, along with initial revenue contribution from heat spreader products. Our new TP 
of TWD327 is based on an unchanged multiple of 28x 2027F EPS of TWD11.68 (previously 28x 
2H26-1H27F EPS of TWD8.96), vs SDI’s historical average of 22.5x since 2015 (a range of 5.6x 
to 70.6x). The stock currently trades at 20x/16x 2027/28F EPS.

### Page 3

Company profile
SDI is a leading global semiconductor lead frame supplier headquartered in Taiwan. The company is the largest power semiconductor lead frame
manufacturer in the world, with its primary focus on automotive, industrial and AI applications.
Valuation Methodology
Our target price of TWD327 is based on 28x 2027F EPS of TWD11.68 vs historical average of 22.5x since 2015 with a range of 5.6x to 70.6x. The
benchmark for is this stock is TAIEX.

Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price
Downside risks: 1) slower-than-expected auto, industrial, and consumer electronic demand recovery, 2) delays of new products' mass production, 3)
pricing competition from global peers, and 4) dramatic copper price drop in short periods, which should be a negative impact on SDI's near-term GM.
ESG
With the core philosophy of “sincerity, stability, challenge, and innovation,” SDI is dedicated to environmental protection, social responsibility,

respecting human rights, and transparent corporate governance

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

1 
6907 雅特力-KY 
(Artery Technology Corp.)

報告日期： 2026/09/11 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、營 運 成 長

一、雅特力-KY 為專注 32 位元研發設計廠商，產品涵蓋
工控、車用與物聯網應用 
雅特力-KY(6907)為從事 32 位元微控制器(Microcontroller Unit，
MCU)之研發設計與銷售業務，產品廣泛應用於工業與馬達控制、
消費性電子、能源管理、商務設備、車載電子及物聯網等，雅特
力-KY 2Q26 產品應用別，Smart Home 佔 18%、AioT 佔 25%、Smart 
Industrial 佔 16%、Smart Mobility 佔 19%、Smart Business 佔 16%、
Others 佔 6% 。 雅特力-KY 之國際競爭對手為 STM 、 Microchip 、 TI、
NXP 、 Renesas 等廠商， 於大中華區之同業為中穎電子 、兆易創新、
芯海、新唐、松翰、九齊及應廣等。MCU 產業因應市場上的價格
競爭，刺激市場的補貼政策，營運自 2025 年起毛利率開始回穩，
顯示降價以去化庫存的影響性已降低，MCU 報價在數個季度調整
與中國廠商價格競爭壓力趨緩之下，價格壓力已經逐步減緩。展
望 MCU 市況，受到上游晶圓代工與後段封測的成本持續攀升，為
反應成本攀升、原物料(其中，包含被動元件電阻電容、塑料)等零
組件成本續增，MCU 於 2026 年 4 月、7 月逐步反應報價，主要反
應與轉嫁成本續增的壓力；雅特力-KY 成立初期即對標 32bits 
ARM-Based M4 以上產品線 ， 特點為 Memory Embeded(SRAM 、 Flash)
受到記憶體缺貨料源不足影響較少 。 雅特力-KY 的 32bit MCU 初期
與國際大廠 STMicro 相容(Compatible)設計，相容性高的產品，容
易切入市場量產，品項多元供應更具有彈性，避開與陸系廠商之
8bit、16bit、低階 M0 以下 32bit 相對紅海市場的競爭。

二、2026 年機器人市場需求提升，可望帶動營運成長 
雅特力-KY 擁有完整的馬達控制演算法與解決方案，是其關鍵的
競爭力。展望 2026 年，雅特力-KY 會聚焦於車載市場應用發展，
當前之車載應用領域，概略分為前裝和後裝市場。其中，前裝市
場皆會要求滿足 AEC-Q100 和功能安全認證 ， 而後裝市場則視部件
功能和客戶需求而定，以雅特力-KY 產品為例，多個產品型號均
已通過 AEC-Q100 車規可靠性認證，且於 2026 年初也取得了 ISO 
26262 ASIL-D 等級流程認證。對於雅特力-KY 從車用後裝市場跨
入前裝市場更為有利，亦成為雅特力-KY 進軍 Tier1 車廠之一新的
里程碑，在車載資通訊系統持續滲透車用市場趨勢帶動之下，車
投資建議：買進 
目標價位：230.00 元

用領域對於高階 MCU 用量預期會逐步提升。此外，人機介面整合
趨勢下，未來在人形機器人的手臂與靈巧手方面，致動器所需的
馬達、齒輪及功率電子等關鍵零組件，成為關鍵技術與零組件供
應 。 以雅特力-KY 而言， 當前雅特力-KY 的機器人與機器狗營收佔
比不足 1%、其餘涵蓋掃地機器人、擦窗機器人、割草機器人等廣
義機器人營收比重約 8%，預期 2027 年機器人佔營收比重可望較
4Q26 時期提升 5~6 倍之多，雅特力-KY 之強項在於馬達控制，機
器人關節強調快速反應和精密定位控制，中國機器人系統業者多
採用 MCU ，主訴其功率、扭力與性價比，均需要定位、轉速控
制，而雅特力-KY 的 MCU 設計以 M4 規格為核心，MCU 背後生態
系涵蓋馬達控制、Flash 記憶體容量更大、運算效能速度快、特定
網路通訊協議(CAN-FD)均為雅特力-KY 之強項，雅特力-KY 於電
機演算法之精進與生態系統建立，均樹立起雅特力-KY 在泛用機
器人應用領域佔穩一席之地。此外，機器人發展進程，雅特力-KY
於 2026 年 9 月 8 日，入列 Arm 全面設計(Arm Total Design)生態系
展示中。Arm 擴展至實體 AI(Physical AI)涵蓋 AI 模型、軟體、運
算硬體、感測器、虛擬平台及數位孿生領域，加速機器人、自駕
車等自主系統從概念驗證走向大規模部署。雅特力-KY 之產品，
對應機器人的行為、輸出與系統需求，足以涵蓋延遲、運算配置、
記憶體、功耗、確定性及安全性等指標，其中細項包含，即時控
制指節馬達、處理器編碼、執行角度速度及力矩閉環控制精密度
等功能，針對一雙機器人靈巧手搭載 40 顆+MCU，人形機器人搭
載 50~70 顆 MCU，對於雅特力-KY 產品組合優化均有助益。

三、邊緣型 AI 之 MCU 應用，為市場矚目之成長動能 
為因應邊緣型 AI 市場興起，雅特力-KY 指出在開發邊緣型 AI 應
用時開發環境也是相當重要的一環 ， 一般 MCU 供應商都需要提供
完整 AI 開發環境。2026 年雅特力-KY 將會推出 AI 開發工具，以
簡化建立 AI 模型時需要的資料蒐集和模型訓練工作，訓練完成
後，再利用此 AI 開發模型工具產生經過訓練的模型原始碼，並部
署到 MCU 中進行測試。邊緣型 AI 市場高階應用主要是以 SoC 為
主，用於串流影像處理和大語言模型等場合，如：手機的 SoC 具
備強大算力，可囊括大語言模型、影像處理、影像辨識和語音互
動等強大功能；相比之下 MCU 產品可著力於諸如：加速度計、壓
力、氣體、溫度、圖像等感測器相關應用，涵蓋工業、消費和醫
療應用等領域，例如；工廠中機器異常振動偵測、工具機刀具異
常磨損檢測等。為了進一步提升 MCU 在 AI 運算的效能，一般會
再搭載 N P U (神經網路處理單元)做為協處理器，例如:  A r m  
Ethos-U55，以提升 MCU 在 AI 推論時的效能，並進一步適用於更
多複雜的應用場合，例如：聲音內容與聲紋識別、圖像識別等。
雅特力-KY 已投入開發 28nm 製程之 AI MCU 系列產品，針對特殊

應用之邊緣型 AI 市場專用之 MCU 更具利基優勢。展望 2027 年，  
雅特力-KY 指出 Design-Win 專案量新高，如：機器人靈巧手、3D
打印、電競與日系客戶對接滑鼠鍵盤、二輪電動車於中國東南亞
爆發性成長、廣義機器人案量增加、無人機(5-10 顆 MCU)、工具
機各式應用佔比達 10%；STMicro 一線廠商交貨天期達 50 週；主
流產能供應趨於緊俏等 ， 產業趨勢發展均有利於 2027年雅特力-KY
營運穩健成長。

四、投資建議：買進，目標價 230 元 
雅特力-KY(6907)產品之競爭優勢為專注於 32 位元 MCU 當中屬於
中高階的 M4 規格核心，M4 核心運算效力為 M0 規格之 1.3 倍，大
部份 M0 支援頻率約為 48-64MHz ， 雅特力-KY 產品搭載 M4 核心多
數支援 120MHz 以上時脈，最高可支援至 288MHz，滿足高效能應
用需求。因此，雅特力-KY 產品以中高階市場 32 位元 M4 核心為
主，足以避開過度削價競爭的泛用型 MCU 市場。展望 2026 年，
雅特力-KY 之產品組合在車載、工控與 AI 相關應用逐步提升之
下，可望確立雅特力-KY 之長期營運成長動能，展望 2026 年，預
估雅特力-KY 營收為 35.62 億元 ， +213.81%YoY ， 毛利率為 39.03% ，
稅後淨利為 5.28 億元 ， 稅後 EPS+8.49 元 ； 展望 2027 年 ， 雅特力-KY
營運預估呈現雙位數增長，預估 2 7 年營收為 4 0 . 8 1 億元，
+ 1 4 . 5 7 % Y o Y，毛 利 率 為 4 0 . 7 1 %，稅 後 淨 利 6 . 3 1 億 元，稅 後 E P S + 1 0 . 1 4
元。綜合上述，給予雅特力-KY 投資建議為買進，目標價 230 元
(23.00x2027EPS)。

圖二、雅特力-KY 產品著重工業控制與邊緣型 AI 發展

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

2. 未來 2 年車載工控 MCU，強調感測器與 MCU 多元應用發展 
在兩輪電動車應用中 ， 主要負責馬達控制 、 人機介面(車用儀錶板、
大燈/方向燈控制、蜂鳴器等)MCU 在車載與電動車應用中，主要
負責電池管理系統(BMS) 、馬達驅動控制、人機介面(車用儀錶板、
LED 燈控、蜂鳴器等)和整車控制器(剎車與扭力偵測、行駛模式切
換與通訊協定轉換等、控制區域網路互聯等)，近年來單一車款車
用電子搭載率逐年攀升，MCU 扮演要角足以穩定處理各種訊號和
感測資料，並且及時輸出控制指令，確保騎乘安全、起步順暢與
系統節能等需求。

此外，雅特力-KY 也針對兩輪電動車馬達控制器系列產品整合 
180MHz Cortex-M4，內建高效能運算放大器，提供 128KB 快閃記
憶體 ，符合 OTA(遠端軟體升級需求) ， 非常適合各種兩輪電動車應
用，涵蓋電動自行車、輕型電動摩托車、高階電動摩托車，電動

圖四、公司所處產業鏈與相應之技術研發實力

與機器人相關領域新產品應用逐步開展 
雅特力-KY(6907)產品之競爭 優勢為專注於 32 位元 MCU 當中的
M4 規格，M4 核心運算效力為 M0 規格之 1.3 倍，支援頻率 M0 多
數支援 48-64MHz 、 雅特力-KY 產品搭載 M4 核心多數支援 120MHz
以上時脈，最高可支援至 288MHz 高效能應用，因此，雅特力-KY
產品以中高階市場 32 位元 M4 核心為主，足以避開過度削價競爭
的泛用型 MCU 市場。展望 2026 年，預估雅特力-KY 營收為 35.62
億元，+213.81%YoY，毛利率為 39.03%，稅後淨利為 5.28 億元，
稅後 EPS+8.49 元；展望 2027 年，雅特力-KY 營運預估呈現雙位數
增長，預估 27 年營收為 40.81 億元，+14.57%YoY，毛利率為
40.71%，稅後淨利 6.31 億元，稅後 EPS+10.14 元。

## Document 188: ACCI-Westpac Business Survey Q3 2026 (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/ACCI-Westpac Business Survey Q3 2026 (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ACCI–Westpac
Survey of Industrial Trends
Australian Chamber of Commerce and Industry & Westpac Banking Corporation
260th report September 2026 (survey conducted from 20 July to 3 September 2026)
 Momentum in Australia's manufacturing sector remained sluggish through Q3, with the Westpac-ACCI
Actual Composite edging slightly lower from 50.5 in Q2 to 49.1 in Q3, consistent with a neutral-to-slightly-
contractionary reading.
 Manufacturers are contending with numerous headwinds at present. As the Middle East conflict has continued

to unfold, the combination of elevated inflation, higher interest rates and lingering uncertainty has made for a
more challenging environment for consumer demand and some parts of the construction sector.
 Growth in new orders remained absent in Q3, with a net 2% of respondents reporting a decrease after only a
net 1% reported an increase in Q2. Sluggish demand saw a similar net percentage of manufacturers wind back
production, reduce headcount and decrease overtime. Manufacturers instead chipped away at existing order

backlogs and built up some inventory in anticipation of future increases.
 Spillovers from the Middle East conflict continue to play a significant role when it comes to cost pressures.
A net 48% of respondents reported an increase in average unit costs during Q3, barely changed from the net
51% reporting an increase in Q2. Pressures remain particularly acute in sub-sectors with more direct exposure
to fuel, fertiliser and other supply chain stresses, including machinery and equipment, fabricated and primary

metals, chemicals, wood and furniture.
 Despite persistent cost pressures, the proportion of firms reporting an increase in selling prices fell to a
net 24% in Q3. This remained well below the proportion reporting higher average unit costs, suggesting
manufacturers continue to absorb part of the increase in costs rather than fully passing them on to customers,
placing pressure on margins.
 The Westpac-ACCI Expected Composite rose from 52.7 in Q2 to 57.9 in Q3, indicating improved confidence

in future activity. Stronger expectations for new orders and output suggest manufacturers expect the current
period of soft demand to be relatively short lived.
 However, while manufacturers may see some light at the end of the tunnel for demand and production, they
do not see a significant let-up in cost pressures anytime soon, with a net 43% anticipating a further increase
in average unit costs over the next three months. Ongoing margin squeeze is the baseline view among

manufacturers, contributing to a weaker outlook for profit growth.
 Manufacturers remain somewhat cautious about their investment plans for the year ahead. On balance, a net
14% of firms intend to increase plant and equipment spending over the next twelve months, while a net 4%
intend to increase building investment. While expectations have turned more bullish in some areas, until such
outcomes materialise, some level of hesitancy may persist.

The Survey of Industrial Trends produced by the Australian
Chamber of Commerce and Industry & Westpac Banking
Corporation is a quarterly publication.
It is Australia's longest running business survey dating
from 1966, providing a timely update on manufacturing and

insights into economy-wide trends.

Net response or "balance" is calculated by the proportion of
"ups" less "downs" on individual questions, thereby yielding
the net balance. A positive balance indicates a net upward
or improving trend and a minus balance a net downward or
deteriorating trend.

The 260th consecutive survey was closed on Monday, x
September 2026 A total of 413 responses were received,
and provided a reasonable cross–section of Australian
manufacturing in respect of industry groups and size of
operation.

3
Key Survey Results
September 2026
• Manufacturing sentiment about the general business
outlook deteriorated sharply last quarter, as manufacturers
were confronted with acute cost pressures and a stalling in
new orders growth.
• Sentiment remains pessimistic but conditions improved

in Q3, with a net 12% of respondents expect the general
business situation to deteriorate over the next six months,
compared to a net 21% anticipating a deterioration in Q2.
• While manufacturers are expecting an improvement in
demand and production near-term, the backdrop of rising
costs, higher interest rates and difficulties in sourcing
labour will continue to present challenges for businesses.
• Momentum in Australia's manufacturing sector remained

sluggish in Q3, with the Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite
edging slightly lower from 50.5 to 49.1, consistent with a
neutral-to-slightly-contractionary reading.
• Underpinning the result were small declines across all of
the constituent components: output, new orders, overtime,
numbers employed and order backlogs.
• In contrast, the Westpac-ACCI Expected Composite
rose from 52.7 in Q2 to 57.9 in Q3, indicating improved
confidence in future activity. However, recent history has

shown that expectations can run ahead of actual future
outcomes for a period of time, and that could remain the
case should current headwinds persist.
• The Labour Market Composite held steady moving into the
second half of the year, nudging only slightly lower from
44.7 in Q2 to 44.5 in Q3.
• Results in the Labour Market Composite have foreshadowed
the trends in official labour market data for the market
sector, where jobs growth started to recover last year and

kick into a higher gear earlier this year.
• More recently, the Labour Market Composite has pointed to
a renewed slowing in jobs growth momentum. This would
be consistent with a moderation in labour demand over the
coming quarters as the effects of slower economic growth
from high inflation and interest rates continue to weigh on
the activity.

4
Manufacturing & the Business Cycle
• The Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite has a solid track
record as a timely indicator of near-term domestic economic
conditions and turning points in the cycle.
• The Q2 National Accounts showed that growth is slowing
but not as rapidly as initially feared. Underneath that is a

complex mix of headwinds and tailwinds. There are clear
drags coming from higher interest rates, elevated inflation,
slowing public investment and the housing market (albeit
so far confined to the direct effects from lower housing
turnover). However, these are being offset by tailwinds
related to firmer business investment, particularly in data
centres, energy and related infrastructure.
• This survey suggests softer demand and lingering cost
pressures remain a persistent themes that will continue to

shape momentum in local manufacturing and the domestic
economy over the coming months.
The Business Cycle & Economic Outlook
Manufacturing & Business Investment
• The ACCI-Westpac survey has historically provided a broad
gauge on equipment spending trends in the manufacturing
sector. However, actual spending has persistently under
performed the investment intentions in this survey,
suggesting that some plans are failing to materialise.
• Official data had been turning more constructive, but

momentum looks to have slipped once again. The National
Accounts showed that an investment pulse is underway in
Australia, but it is being chiefly driven by a few sectors –
particularly data centres and renewables. Outside of these
sectors, growth in equipment spending is more subdued.
• Ongoing uncertainty surrounding the Middle East conflict,
together with elevated cost pressures, higher interest
rates and a subdued demand outlook are likely to remain a

headwind for investment.
Australian & World Manufacturing Surveys
• Global and Australian manufacturing cycles tend to be
synchronised, especially at major turning points. The
Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite typically moves in line
with global manufacturing conditions, albeit with some
level of variation within any given cycle.
• However, more recently, trends have started to diverge
across the major manufacturing hubs. The US continues to
weather the headwinds associated with tariff uncertainty

and the US-Iran war, with the ISM PMI at 54.6 in August,
firmly in expansionary territory. It is a similar story in
Europe, where the S&P Global PMI has risen to 52.7 in
August, its highest in many years. Meanwhile, China's
manufacturing remains relatively lacklustre, similar to
Australia, with the NBS PMI around breakeven territory at
49.8 as of August.

Activity & Orders
• Growth in new orders remained sluggish in Q3, with a net
2% of respondents reporting a decrease, easing from a net
1% reporting an increase in Q2.
• As the Middle East conflict continues, the combination
of elevated inflation, higher interest rates and lingering

uncertainty has made for a more challenging environment
for consumer demand and some parts of the construction
sector.
• Manufacturers expect growth in new orders to improve
over the next three months, with a net 20% anticipating an
increase. Whether these expectations will be met given the
challenging backdrop is a key question.

Exports
Q2 2026 Q3 2026
Actual – net balance 9 8

• Growth in manufacturing exports were broadly stable
moving into the second half of the year, with a net 8% of
firms reporting an increase in Q3, following Q2's net 9%.
• Demand from our Asian trading partners have continued to
support processed food and agriculture exports. However,
a firmer AUD and uncertainty from the Middle East conflict

would have dampened growth in the quarter. US tariff risks
meanwhile remain a wildcard, although fortunately, it has
had little overall impact to date.
• Expectations have cooled compared to earlier in the year
but they remain positive, with a net 12% expecting an
increase in exports over the next three months.
• After slowing materially last quarter, output took another
step lower in Q3. A net 2% of firms reported a decrease in
output, the first decline in a year.

• Demand remained sluggish during the quarter, leading
manufacturers to chip away at existing order backlogs and
build up some inventory in anticipation of future increases.
• Looking ahead, a net 26% of firms expect an increase in
output over the next three months, suggesting the current
soft patch is expected to only be temporary. However, it is
important to note that expectations have been more bullish
than actual future outcomes over the past two years.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
• Manufacturers turned more cautious about their investment
plans for the year ahead, following the onset of the Middle
East conflict.
• That broadly remained the case in Q3, though investment
intentions were still positive. On balance, a net 14% of
firms intend to increase plant and equipment spending over

the next twelve months, while a net 4% intend to increase
building investment.
• There remains a significant degree of uncertainty around
the economic outlook. Expectations for production and
orders have turned more bullish, but until such outcomes
materialise, some level of hesitancy might persist.
Investment & Profitability
• Profit expectations took a substantial hit last quarter,
reflecting a combination of surging cost pressures, higher
interest rates and the onset of the Middle East conflict.

This saw a sharp increase in cost pressures and substantial
uncertainty around the outlook.
• With these headwinds persisting, the outlook for profits
was little changed. Only a net 10% of firms expect an
improvement in profits over the coming year, below the
historic average of a net 19%.
• While manufacturers are anticipating an improvement in
new orders over the coming months, cost pressures are
also expected to remain acute, compressing margins.

• Capacity utilisation has quickly swung from a period of
overutilisation earlier this year, prior to the onset of the
Middle East conflict, back toward underutilisation.
• In the latest survey, fewer firms reported operating at
above average capacity (23% to 18%) with more firms
reporting that they were operating at below average
capacity (25% to 28%). This saw an increase in the net
balance of firms reporting capacity underutilisation, up from
2% in Q2 to 10% in Q3.

• This is still a fairly robust result. Over the full history
of the survey, manufacturers have tended to report a
larger degree of underutilisation, especially during cycles
characterised by slower growth and/or higher interest
rates.

• Employment dynamics in the manufacturing sector remain
sluggish. A net 2% of firms reported a decline in the size
of the workforce in Q3, following on from a net 4% also
reporting a pull-back in Q2.
• Manufacturers were confronted with a stagnation in new
orders growth and pull-back in production during the

quarter, providing little scope to increase headcount.
• Over the next three months however, a net 7% of
firms expect to increase headcount. While modest, the
expected lift in employment coincides with manufacturers'
expectations for a rebound in new orders and production
over the coming months.
The Labour Market
• Historically, firms' views on the difficulty of finding labour
tracks shifts in the Australian unemployment rate, albeit
with some variation between cycles depending on the

industry mix of employment growth.
• In Q3, a larger share of manufacturers reported difficulty
in sourcing labour, with a net 28.3% indicating labour was
"harder to find" compared to three months ago.
• Overall jobs growth has held up well so far this year, but
the spill overs from the Middle East conflict and recent
interest rate rises are expected to weigh on jobs growth
over the coming months.
• Manufacturers' use of overtime deteriorated further in Q3,

with a net 3% reporting a decline, in line with expectations
from the prior quarter.
• Hours worked usually adjusts more quickly to shifts in
demand conditions than employment. The fact that both
eased materially at the same time points to a more abrupt
cooling in labour demand within the sector.
• Manufacturers anticipate a modest improvement in
overtime usage over the coming three months, with a net
5% expecting an increase. If manufacturers' expectations

for demand crystallise, some increase in overtime would be
required to avoid building up order backlogs.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
8
• Despite persistent unit cost pressures, the proportion of
firms reporting an increase in selling prices fell slightly
during Q3, to a net 24%.
• This was well below the net 48% reporting an increase in
average unit costs over the same period. It also fell well
short of manufacturers' own expectations from last quarter,

implying margins are being squeezed as firms only partially
pass through higher costs onto customers.
• Manufacturers expect only partial pass-through of higher
costs to customers will remain the case over the next
few months, with a net 27% expecting to increase selling
prices.
Prices & Inflation
• Results around wage expectations in the manufacturing
sector were broadly unchanged versus last quarter, with a
net 31% indicating they expect their next enterprise wage

agreement to delivery an outcome above the last.
• The survey suggests no real step-change in expectations
following the Fair Work Commission's 2026 Annual Wage
Review, which announced a larger-than-expected 4.75%
increase in award wages in Q2, to be implemented during
Q3. Expectations are still elevated, and this may compound
existing non-labour cost pressures for many manufacturers.
• Consistent with this survey, official ABS data points to a
turnaround in the wages growth trend, up to 3.3%yr in Q2,

slightly outstripping the national average of 3.2%yr.
• Manufacturers are grappling with acute cost pressures as
the supply shock stemming from the Middle East continues
to impact. In Q3, a net 48% of firms reported a rise in
average unit costs.
• Sub-sectors with more direct exposure to fuel, energy
intensive goods such as fertilisers, plastics and metals are
reporting more acute pressure, including machinery and
equipment, fabricated and primary metals, chemicals, wood

and furniture.
• Manufacturers do not see a significant let-up in cost
pressures near-term, with a net 43% anticipating a further
increase in average unit costs over the next three months.

Factors Limiting Production
Single Factor Most Limiting Production
Q1 2026 Q2 2026 Q3 2026
Orders (%) 29 35 36
Capacity (%) 11 10 8
Labour (%) 24 17 20
Finance (%)   12 10 12
Materials (%) 12 14 8
Other (%) 4 5 5
None (%) 8 9 11
• Orders remain an important constraint on production – a

lack of demand saw 36% of respondents cite this as the
single factor most limiting production. However, this is still
well below historical averages, showcasing the relative
importance of other supply-side limitations currently.
• Labour remains a clear example, with 20% citing it as the
primary constraint in Q3, above Q2's 17% and well above
the pre-pandemic average. Difficulties in sourcing labour
remains a persistent theme.
• Material shortages appear to have eased, with the

proportion of respondents citing it as the most limiting
factor down from 14% to 8%. While off its recent highs, it
is still well above the pre-pandemic average.
• Capacity dipped slightly to 8% in Q3, but the proportion
of respondents citing None has steadily risen over recent
quarters, up to 11% in Q3, suggesting that despite recent
disruptions and volatility, a decent portion of manufacturers'
production is returning back to some kind of 'norm'.

The Westpac–ACCI Composite Indices
The Westpac–ACCI Actual and Expected Composite indices are weighted averages of the activity measures in the survey. The 
weights are as follows: employment 20%; new orders 30%; output 25%; order backlog 15%; overtime 10% .
The Westpac–ACCI Labour Market Composite is a function of actual employment, with a weight of almost 50%, as well as: 
expected employment; expected overtime; new orders; order backlog; and expected order backlog.

MACRO STRATEGY 

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK 
MARKETS IN REVIEW 
Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.

For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to

## Document 232: CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng Không Taseco - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng Không Taseco - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-22).pdf

### Page 1

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trưởng tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác 
tăng bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế 
gia tăng được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 
182 điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp từ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất và 6 điểm mới tại 
T2 Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Quốc và Gia Bình tiếp tục mở 
ra dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh được kỳ vọng 
tác động tích cực lên biên lợi nhuận của AST. Chúng tôi dự phóng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp đạt 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 
48%/17% yoy.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với AST, giá mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco là một 
trong những doanh nghiệp có quy mô mạng 
lưới điểm kinh doanh lớn trong lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ phi hàng không tại Việt Nam, với 141 
điểm kinh doanh tại 6 sân bay quốc tế tính 
đến cuối tháng 6/2026. Hoạt động cốt lõi của 
công ty bao gồm bán lẻ bách hóa lưu niệm 
(BHLN), hàng miễn thuế, dịch vụ ăn uống và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Ngoài ra, AST còn 
tham gia lĩnh vực suất ăn hàng không thông 
qua khoản đầu tư tại công ty liên kết VinaCS. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Doanh thu trên các điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu tăng nhờ lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa dự phóng tăng 
11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng trưởng mới, với số điểm kinh doanh AST 2026 dự kiến đạt 145 
điểm và tăng lên 182 điểm vào 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Biên lợi nhuận gộp cải thiện lên 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027 nhờ mở rộng hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ và tối ưu vận hành.

với động lực: (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ 
VND (+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Biểu đồ 5. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh của AST

Nguồn:AST

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng tăng trưởng lưu lượng hành khách, đặc biệt là sự gia tăng tỷ 
trọng khách quốc tế, sẽ thúc đẩy doanh thu và hiệu quả kinh doanh của AST 
trong giai đoạn 2026–2030. Theo Cục Hàng không Việt Nam, lượng khách thông 
qua các cảng hàng không trong 7T2026 đạt hơn 76 triệu lượt (+7% yoy), trong 
đó khách quốc tế đạt gần 30 triệu lượt (+11% yoy), trong khi khách nội địa ghi 
nhận tăng trưởng 4% yoy. Xu hướng này được hỗ trợ bởi nhu cầu du lịch quốc 
tế, chính sách thị thực thuận lợi và việc mở rộng mạng bay, trong khi tăng 
trưởng kinh tế và thu nhập tiếp tục tạo nền tảng cho nhu cầu đi lại trong nước. 
Trên cơ sở đó, chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế và nội địa tại các thị 
trường AST khai thác tăng bình quân lần lượt 11% và 5%/năm đến 2030, qua đó 
nâng dần tỷ trọng khách quốc tế trong tổng lưu lượng, thúc đẩy doanh 
thu/điểm kinh doanh nhờ: (1) lưu lượng hành khách qua cảng hàng không gia 
tăng và (2) tỷ trọng khách quốc tế cao hơn hỗ trợ doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

lượt) 
 Biểu đồ 7. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh AST (tỷ VND)

Nguồn: AST  Nguồn: AST

Mở rộng mạng lưới tại các nhà ga 
hiện hữu và sân bay mới, gia tăng 
quy mô kinh doanh đến 2030 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng chu kỳ mở rộng hạ tầng hàng không giai đoạn 2026–2030 sẽ 
tạo dư địa gia tăng quy mô kinh doanh cho AST, với số điểm kinh doanh dự 
phóng đạt 145 điểm vào cuối 2026 và tăng lên 182 điểm vào năm 2030. Cụ thể: 
- Trong ngắn hạn, tăng trưởng năm 2026 được hỗ trợ bởi việc ghi nhận 
đủ năm doanh thu từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
trong năm 2025, cùng 6 điểm mới tại T2 Nội Bài trong năm 2026, trong 
đó có 2 cửa hàng miễn thuế và 2 cửa hàng Memories of Vietnam khai 
trương trong 2Q2026. 
- Trong trung và dài hạn, các dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành, Cảng HKQT 
Phú Quốc nâng cấp mở rộng và Cảng HKQT Gia Bình sẽ góp phần thúc 
đẩy ngành hàng không Việt Nam nói chung, bổ sung mặt bằng thương 
mại, mở ra cơ hội phát triển các mảng kinh doanh cốt lõi cho AST nói 
riêng bao gồm các mảng bán lẻ, miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Với kinh nghiệm khai thác 141 điểm kinh doanh 
tại 6 sân bay quốc tế và lịch sử trúng thầu tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất, T2 Nội 
Bài trong thời gian qua, chúng tôi kỳ vọng AST có khả năng cạnh tranh 
cao trong việc đấu thầu quyền khai thác mặt bằng tại các sân bay mới, 
đặc biệt là Gia Bình, qua đó duy trì vị thế và thị phần kinh doanh phi 
hàng không tại khu vực phía Bắc khi một phần lưu lượng hành khách 
được phân bổ lại từ Nội Bài.

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ mở rộng biên lợi nhuận  
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng biên lợi nhuận của AST tiếp tục cải thiện trong 2026 - 2027, 
nhờ định hướng mở rộng các mảng có biên lợi nhuận tốt như hàng miễn thuế 
và phòng chờ thương gia, kết hợp tối ưu hiệu quả vận hành tại các điểm kinh 
doanh. Cụ thể: (1) AST ưu tiên gia tăng số điểm kinh doanh hàng miễn thuế 
nhằm đón đầu tăng trưởng nhanh và ổn định của lượng khách quốc tế, qua đó 
nâng đóng góp lợi nhuận từ hoạt động mua sắm tại sân bay, (2) mở rộng mạng 
lưới phòng chờ, đồng thời gia tăng lượng khách sử dụng trên nền năng lực phục 
vụ hiện hữu và (3) tối ưu hóa hiệu quả hoạt động thông qua cải thiện bố trí mặt 
bằng, danh mục sản phẩm và kiểm soát chi phí tại các điểm kinh doanh, qua 
đó tăng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thu được trên mỗi khách hàng.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá 
mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu AST, với giá mục tiêu 76,500 
VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 
21/09/2026, dựa trên triển vọng tăng trưởng từ lượng khách quốc tế, mở rộng 
mạng lưới điểm kinh doanh và khả năng cải thiện biên lợi nhuận của doanh 
nghiệp. Chúng tôi sử dụng phương pháp định giá từng phần (SOTP), bao gồm 
DCF cho hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi của AST và P/E mục tiêu 11x đối với phần 
lợi nhuận thuộc sở hữu AST tại VinaCS. Các giả định chính gồm: (1) lượng khách 
quốc tế/nội địa tăng trung bình 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030, (2) số điểm kinh 
doanh tăng từ 145 điểm cuối 2026 lên 182 điểm năm 2030, với giả định AST 
trúng thầu mặt bằng tại cả Long Thành và Gia Bình và (3) biên lợi nhuận gộp 
2026/2027 đạt 64.7/65.5%. Đối với VinaCS, chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhà máy suất ăn 
Long Thành sẽ hỗ trợ tăng trưởng lợi nhuận trong trung và dài hạn khi dự kiến 
có thể bắt đầu đóng góp lợi nhuận cho AST từ 2028.

Rủi ro đối với định giá bao gồm: (1) AST không giành được quyền khai thác tại 
Long Thành và Gia Bình như kỳ vọng, (2) lưu lượng khách dịch chuyển giữa các 
sân bay mới và hiện hữu khiến doanh thu tại một số điểm kinh doanh bị sụt 
giảm, (3) cạnh tranh gia tăng từ các doanh nghiệp kinh doanh dịch vụ phi hàng 
không khác và (4) căng thẳng địa chính trị kéo dài, giá dầu tăng gây áp lực lên 
hoạt động hàng không và chi tiêu của hành khách.

## Document 247: Energy Bills_ Short-term relief, longer-term challenges Utility bill inflation has moderated, but household payments remain elevated and longer-term pressures are building. 2 min r

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy Bills_ Short-term relief, longer-term challenges Utility bill inflation has moderated, but household payments remain elevated and longer-term pressures are building. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13018305     1

Economy 
Energy bills: Short-term relief, longer-term challenges 
16 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Energy bill inflation was around 4% year-over-year (YoY) in August 2026. However, Bank of America internal deposit data shows 
average utility bill payments rose faster, increasing 5% YoY from June through August. This was likely due, in part, to higher 
cooling demand during a warm summer. 
• But bill pressures remain highly uneven across the country. Average utility payments have risen by more than 10% YoY in cities 
such as Detroit, Baltimore and Washington DC, while consumers in Orlando, San Jose and Tampa have seen declines. Some of 
these regional differences appear to reflect policy and infrastructure factors as much as energy usage itself. 
• The long-term outlook still points to upward pressure on electricity bills as demand from data center construction, electrification 
and broader economic activity drive the need for additional generation and grid investment. However, a forecasted strong El 
Niño event this winter could provide some near-term relief, if warmer-than-normal temperatures reduce household heating 
demand, partially offsetting structural cost pressures.

Energy bill inflation has moderated  
Energy is an essential household expense. According to Bureau of Labor Statistics data, energy bills accounted for roughly 3% of 
total household spending as of 2024. US utility prices have been far less affected by higher global oil prices stemming from the 
conflict in the Middle East than gasoline prices, largely because the US is a net exporter of natural gas. Nonetheless, the cost of 
energy has been rising for a prolonged period: since January 2024 the price of electricity has risen 10% and the price of piped 
gas is up 18%.  
The better news is that utility price inflation has moderated relative to the second half of 2025 and early 2026. As of August, 
electricity and piped gas prices were up around 4% year-over-year (YoY). But Bank of America internal deposit data shows that 
average utility payments for electricity and gas were running ahead of inflation in these categories, up 5.3% YoY. One reason 
could be that household demand for cooling was strong this summer, due to relatively warm weather (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Utility bill payments rose around 5.3% YoY in August  
Consumer prices for electricity and piped gas (% YoY) and three-month average monthly utility payments per customer based on Bank of America internal 
deposit data (% YoY)

Source: Haver Analytics, Bank of America internal data

### Page 2

2 16 September 2026

I NSTI TUTE

Energy costs vary widely by region 
While energy bills account for a relatively modest share of household spending nationally, experiences vary across the country. 
Looking at large cities (by Core Based Statistical Areas), our data shows that utility bills rose by more than 10% YoY in Detroit, 
Baltimore and Washington DC from June through August 2026. At the other end of the spectrum, bills fell YoY in Orlando, San 
Joe and Tampa (Exhibit 2).  
Customers’ bills are being pressured by reshoring, reindustrialization, the general pass-through costs in the economy, and a 
significant need for infrastructure replacement in many markets. Regulatory changes and one-off charges have also influenced 
costs in several areas. In Florida, for example, the removal of hurricane recovery surcharges reduced utility bills in 2026, while in 
the Northeast, investments in grid infrastructure and capacity have contributed to higher costs.  
There also remains some disparities in where average bills sit relative to the overall US (Exhibit 3). In Washington DC, Boston, 
Phoenix and Dallas, the average utility bill payment in June through August 2026 was well above the US average. Conversely, 
Chicago, Nashville and Las Vegas paid some of the lowest energy bills relative to the US.  
Exhibit 2: There is a fairly wide dispersion of utility bill growth 
across the country

Rising demand for electricity will continue to pressure bills  
Looking longer-term, rising energy demand (particularly for electricity), driven by factors such as data center expansion and 
residential electrification, is likely to keep upward pressure on consumers’ energy bills for a number of years (read Is the AI 
revolution raising consumers' utility bills? and Can the AI buildout keep building? for more).  
Exhibit 4 shows forecasts from the US Energy Information Administration’s August 2026 Short-term Energy Outlook, estimating 
a 4% YoY rise in commercial and industrial electricity consumption. Accommodating this growth will likely require ongoing 
investment in grid capacity and power generation, and some of these costs may be passed on to consumers through higher 
energy bills.  
Exhibit 4: The demand for electricity is rising particularly from commercial and industrial sectors 
US electrical demand (in billion kilowatt hours) 2020-2027E (estimates/forecast from 2026)

Source: US Energy Information Administration 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Short-term, the weather could prove important  
El Niño is the warming phase of ENSO (El Niño–Southern Oscillation), a global climate phenomenon that emerges from variation 
in winds and sea surface temperatures over the Pacific Ocean. El Niño events can have widespread and profound global impacts, 
causing extreme weather such as storms, floods and higher temperatures. The strongest recorded El Niño took place in 1877 to 
1878 and led to a global famine that killed more than 50 million people.  
The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) believes there is a high probability that a very strong El Niño 
event will occur this year (Exhibit 5). One way to put this in historical context is through the NOAA’s Relative Oceanic Niño Index 
(RONI). A positive RONI indicates the central/eastern tropical Pacific is unusually warm relative to the rest of the tropics. A 
higher RONI indicates a more severe El Niño, though NOAA points out that uncertainty on impacts also tends to rise with RONI.  
Exhibit 5: The NOAA attaches a very high probability to a severe El Niño event over coming months  
Likelihood of an El Nino event estimated by severity (%)

Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA)

Exhibit 6 shows that the current forecast for RONI from October to December 2026 is one of the largest on record. In recent 
history, similar-sized El Niño events have only occurred in 1997-98, 2015-16 and to a lesser extent in 2022-23.  
Exhibit 6: The El Niño event in 2026 is forecasted to be the largest on record 
Relative Oceanic Niño Index (RONI) for October-December by year (2026: NOAA 50% probability forecast for October-December 2026)

Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) 
Note: Red bars: 1997-1998/2015-16. Yellow bar: 2026-27 50% forecast. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
For the US, significant El Niño events tend to bring higher-than-usual temperatures and more rainfall (Exhibit 7). However, no El 
Niño is exactly the same. For example, in 1997-98 California was flooded in but in 2015-16 it was dry, while the nation overall 
was wet. Both time periods were among the highest levels of RONI in the past 70 years.  
Exhibit 7: El Niño events have been noted for higher temperatures and rainfall  
Average winter (December-February) temperatures versus precipitation (data from 1996-97)

Source: Haver Analytics

### Page 5
